\medterm{Autosomal Inheritance} Transmission of a genetic trait through one of the 22 non-sex chromosomes. Autosomal
dominant traits may appear in successive generations, whereas autosomal recessive
disease generally requires pathogenic variants on both homologous chromosomes.

\medterm{Autosomal Recessive PKD} Rare childhood disorder from PKHD1 mutation. Bilateral enlarged kidneys with fusiform
dilation of collecting ducts. Associated with congenital hepatic fibrosis. Severe forms
cause pulmonary hypoplasia in utero. Variable severity from perinatal lethal to
presentation in childhood or adolescence.

\medterm{Avascular Necrosis} Death of bone tissue caused by impaired blood supply. It commonly affects the hip or shoulder and
may cause pain, collapse of the joint surface, and arthritis. Risk factors include
corticosteroid exposure, alcohol use, trauma, sickle-cell disease, and systemic lupus
erythematosus. Treatment depends on the site and stage and may include observation,
joint-preserving surgery, or joint replacement.

\medterm{Avascular Necrosis (Osteonecrosis)} Death of bone tissue caused by impaired blood supply. It commonly affects the femoral
head and may lead to subchondral collapse, secondary arthritis, and need for
joint-preserving surgery or arthroplasty.

\synonyms
Osteonecrosis

\medterm{Avian Influenza} Influenza caused by avian-adapted influenza A viruses, acquired mainly through contact
with infected birds or contaminated environments. Human infection can cause severe
pneumonia, respiratory failure, and occasionally systemic disease.

\medterm{Avoidant Personality Disorder} A pattern of social inhibition, feelings of inadequacy, and hypersensitivity to negative
evaluation.

\medterm{Avolition} A severe lack of motivation to initiate and perform self-directed, purposeful
activities. It is a negative symptom of schizophrenia and is seen in severe depression.
It manifests as neglect of personal hygiene, inability to complete tasks, and social
withdrawal.

\medterm{Avulsed Tooth} A tooth completely displaced from its socket by trauma. It is a dental emergency;
management depends on patient age, extraoral dry time, storage medium, root development,
and periodontal-ligament viability, and may require urgent replantation by a dental
professional.

\medterm{Avulsion} An injury in which tissue is forcibly torn from the body by shearing or pulling. Common
forms include dental, fingertip, nail, and degloving avulsions. Management focuses on
hemorrhage control, debridement, tissue preservation, and urgent consultation.

\medterm{Avulsion Fracture} A fracture in which a fragment of bone is pulled away by the tendon or ligament attached
to it, usually during sudden forceful contraction or injury. Common sites include the
pelvis, ankle, elbow, and fingers; evaluation considers displacement, joint involvement,
skeletal maturity, and associated soft-tissue injury.

\medterm{Awake Fiberoptic Intubation} An intubation technique performed while the patient remains responsive and breathing
spontaneously, using topical airway anesthesia and a flexible bronchoscope. It is one option
when difficult airway management is anticipated and maintaining oxygenation is important.

\medterm{Awareness Under Anesthesia} Unintended awareness or recall of events during general anesthesia. It may involve
hearing, sensation, or pain and can be associated with significant psychological distress after the procedure.

\medterm{Axilla} Pyramidal space between the upper lateral thorax and medial arm. Bounded by anterior
fold (pectoralis major/minor), posterior fold (latissimus dorsi/teres major), medial
wall (ribs/serratus anterior), and lateral wall (humerus). Contains axillary
artery/vein, brachial plexus cords, and lymph nodes.

\medterm{Axillary Artery} The continuation of the subclavian artery, running from the outer border of the first
rib to the lower border of teres major, where it becomes the brachial artery. Divided
into three parts by the pectoralis minor.

\medterm{Axillary Nerve} From posterior cord (C5-C6), passing through the quadrangular space with the posterior
circumflex humeral artery. Innervates deltoid and teres minor; provides sensation to the
regimental badge area (lateral deltoid skin). Vulnerable in anterior shoulder
dislocations and surgical neck humeral fractures.

\medterm{Axis} Second cervical vertebra with the dens (odontoid process) projecting superiorly, serving
as a pivot for atlas rotation, allowing approximately 50 percent of cervical rotation.
Has a large spinous process and flat superior articular facets.

\medterm{Axon} The single long process of a neuron that conducts action potentials away from the cell
body toward the axon terminal for synaptic transmission.

\medterm{Azole Antifungals} A class of antifungal agents that inhibit the enzyme lanosterol 14-alpha-demethylase, a
cytochrome P450 enzyme essential for ergosterol synthesis in fungal cell membranes. The
depletion of ergosterol and accumulation of toxic methylated sterols disrupt membrane
integrity and function.

\medterm{Azoospermia} Absence of spermatozoa in the ejaculate after appropriate centrifugation and microscopic
examination. It may be obstructive, with sperm production preserved but outflow blocked,
or nonobstructive, with impaired spermatogenesis.

\medterm{Azygos Vein} A vein that drains the posterior intercostal veins and empties into the superior vena
cava. It forms an important collateral pathway when the SVC is obstructed. On chest
imaging, enlargement of the azygos vein may indicate SVC obstruction, heart failure, or
portal hypertension.

\medterm{Azygous vein} A large vein that drains the posterior thoracic wall and mediastinum, ascending along
the right side of the vertebral column to empty into the superior vena cava. It provides
collateral venous return when the superior or inferior vena cava is obstructed.

\medterm{Aarskog Syndrome} A rare inherited disorder affecting growth, facial development, the skeleton, and sometimes the genitals.

\medterm{Acute Kidney Failure} A sudden decline in kidney function that causes waste products and fluid to accumulate in the body.

\medterm{Addison's Disease} A disorder in which the adrenal glands produce too little cortisol and, often, too little aldosterone.

\medterm{Adrenal Cortex} The outer part of an adrenal gland that produces steroid hormones, including cortisol and aldosterone.

\medterm{Aortic Valve Insufficiency} Leakage of the aortic valve that allows blood to flow back into the left ventricle.

\medterm{Aortic Valve Stenosis} Narrowing of the aortic valve that obstructs blood flow from the left ventricle into the aorta.

\medterm{Aortitis} Inflammation of the aortic wall.

\medterm{Arachnoiditis} Inflammation and scarring of the arachnoid membrane and nearby spinal nerves, often causing chronic pain or neurological symptoms.

\medterm{Atrioventricular Block} Delayed or interrupted electrical conduction between the atria and ventricles of the heart.

\medterm{5-alpha Reductase} An enzyme that converts testosterone to dihydrotestosterone, a hormone involved in prostate growth and male sexual development.

\medterm{Abdominoplasty} A surgical procedure that removes excess abdominal skin and may tighten the abdominal wall. It is commonly called a tummy tuck.

\synonyms
a tummy tuck.

\medterm{Ablation} Destruction or removal of a small area of abnormal tissue using methods such as heat, cold, chemicals, or electrical energy.

\medterm{Abutment} A tooth or implant that supports and anchors a dental prosthesis.

\medterm{Accommodation} The ability of the eye to change the shape of its lens so that near objects can be focused clearly.

\medterm{Acetaldehyde} A chemical produced when the body metabolizes alcohol. Accumulation may contribute to flushing, nausea, and vomiting.

\medterm{Achilles Tendon} The strong tendon connecting the calf muscles to the heel bone. It can become inflamed or rupture.

\medterm{Acupressure} Application of pressure to selected body points, usually to relieve symptoms such as pain or nausea.

\medterm{Acupuncture} A treatment in which thin needles are inserted into selected points of the body.

\medterm{Acute Pain} Pain that begins suddenly and usually lasts for a limited period, often because of injury, illness, or a procedure.

\medterm{Acute Urinary Retention} Sudden inability to empty the bladder, often causing painful lower-abdominal distension and requiring urgent assessment.

\medterm{Adenosine Triphosphate} A molecule that stores and transfers energy within cells. It is commonly abbreviated ATP.

\medterm{Adhesion} A band of scar-like tissue that abnormally connects surfaces or organs inside the body.

\medterm{Advance Care Directive} A legal document stating a person's preferences for medical care if the person later cannot make or communicate decisions.

\medterm{AIDS Dementia} A disorder of thinking, memory, behavior, or movement caused by advanced HIV-related disease affecting the brain.

\medterm{Accident and Emergency Medicine (A\&E Medicine)} The medical specialty concerned with the immediate assessment, resuscitation, diagnosis, and initial treatment of acute illness and injury.

\synonyms
A\&E Medicine

\medterm{Acute Abdomen} Sudden or severe abdominal pain with clinical features suggesting a potentially serious intra-abdominal disorder that may require urgent investigation or surgery.

\medterm{Acetone} A volatile ketone body produced during increased fat metabolism. It may contribute to the characteristic breath odor of ketosis and ketoacidosis.

\medterm{Adenitis} Inflammation of a gland or lymph node, usually caused by infection or an inflammatory disorder. Lymphadenitis is a common form.

\medterm{Adolescence} The developmental period between childhood and adulthood, involving physical maturation, puberty, and major psychological and social changes.

\medterm{Adrenergic Receptors} Cell-surface receptors activated by adrenaline and noradrenaline. Alpha and beta subtypes regulate cardiovascular, respiratory, metabolic, and other physiological responses.

\medterm{Aetiology (Etiology)} The cause or group of causes underlying a disease, injury, or other medical condition.

\synonyms
Etiology

\medterm{Afterpains} Intermittent uterine cramps occurring after childbirth as the uterus contracts and returns toward its pre-pregnancy size. They may be more noticeable during breastfeeding.

\medterm{Agenesis} Complete failure of an organ or body structure to develop, usually because of a congenital developmental abnormality.

\medterm{Albinism} An inherited condition involving reduced or absent melanin production, affecting the skin, hair, and eyes.

\medterm{Alopecia Totalis} Complete loss of hair from the scalp, usually caused by an autoimmune disorder.

\medterm{Alopecia Universalis} Loss of hair from the entire body, including the scalp, eyebrows, eyelashes, and other hair-bearing areas.

\medterm{Alpha Blockers} Medicines that block alpha-adrenergic receptors and may lower blood pressure or improve urine flow in prostate enlargement.

\medterm{Alveoli} Microscopic air sacs in the lungs where oxygen enters the blood and carbon dioxide leaves it.

\medterm{Alzheimer's Disease} A progressive brain disorder causing deterioration of memory, thinking, behavior, and the ability to perform daily activities.

\medterm{Amsler Grid} A simple chart used to detect or monitor distortions and missing areas in central vision, especially in macular disease.

\medterm{Amyloid} An abnormal protein that can accumulate in tissues and impair their function in several diseases.

\medterm{Anagen} The active growth phase of the hair cycle.

\medterm{Analgesia} Absence or reduction of pain without necessarily causing loss of consciousness.

\medterm{Androgen} A sex hormone, such as testosterone, that contributes to male reproductive development and other body functions.

\medterm{Angiotensin I} An inactive precursor that is converted to angiotensin II in the renin-angiotensin system.

\medterm{Angiotensin II} A hormone that narrows blood vessels and promotes sodium retention, thereby increasing blood pressure.

\medterm{Antibody} A protein produced by the immune system that specifically binds to a foreign or abnormal substance.

\medterm{Anticoagulant} A medicine or substance that reduces the blood's ability to form clots.

\medterm{Antidepressant} A medicine used to treat depressive disorders and some other conditions involving mood, anxiety, pain, or sleep.

\medterm{Antiplatelet Agents} Medicines that reduce platelet activation or aggregation and thereby lower the risk of some blood clots.

\medterm{Apolipoproteins} Proteins associated with lipids in the blood that help transport and metabolize cholesterol and triglycerides.

\medterm{Articular Cartilage} Smooth cartilage covering the ends of bones within a joint, allowing low-friction movement and distributing load.

\medterm{Atrophy} Wasting or decrease in the size and function of a tissue, organ, or muscle.

\medterm{Auditory Nerve} The portion of the vestibulocochlear nerve that carries hearing signals from the inner ear to the brain.

\medterm{Aura} A temporary sensory, visual, or other neurological symptom that may precede a migraine or seizure.

\medterm{Autoantibodies} Antibodies that mistakenly react with the body's own cells or tissues.

\medterm{Axon Terminal} The end of a nerve-cell axon that releases chemical messengers to communicate with another cell.

\medterm{Achilles Tendinitis} Painful inflammation or degeneration of the Achilles tendon, usually caused by repeated loading.

\medterm{ACL Injury} A sprain or tear of the anterior cruciate ligament in the knee.

\medterm{Acute Myelogenous Leukemia} A fast-growing cancer of immature myeloid blood cells in the bone marrow and blood; also called acute myeloid leukemia.

\synonyms
acute myeloid leukemia.

\medterm{Acute Radiation Sickness} Illness caused by a high dose of penetrating radiation received over a short time.

\medterm{Airplane Ear} Ear pain, pressure, or temporary hearing loss caused by unequal air pressure across the eardrum during flight.

\medterm{Alcohol Intolerance} An unpleasant reaction to alcohol caused by difficulty metabolizing alcohol or one of its components.

\medterm{Alcohol Poisoning} A life-threatening condition caused by drinking enough alcohol to impair breathing, consciousness, and other vital functions.

\medterm{Alpha-Gal Syndrome} An allergy to the carbohydrate alpha-gal found in most nonhuman mammalian meat and some products derived from mammals.

\medterm{Ampullary Cancer} Cancer arising in the ampulla of Vater, where the bile duct and pancreatic duct empty into the small intestine.

\medterm{Appendix Cancer} Cancer that begins in the appendix, including neuroendocrine tumors, mucinous tumors, and adenocarcinoma.

\medterm{Antibiotic-Associated Diarrhea} Loose or frequent stools that occur during or soon after antibiotic treatment.

\medterm{Asthma Attack} A sudden worsening of asthma caused by increased airway narrowing and inflammation.

\medterm{Atrioventricular Canal Defect} A congenital heart defect involving abnormal development of the septum and atrioventricular valves; also called an atrioventricular septal defect.

\synonyms
an atrioventricular septal defect.

\medterm{Atrioventricular Nodal Reentry Tachycardia} A rapid regular heart rhythm caused by a reentry circuit near the atrioventricular node; commonly abbreviated AVNRT.

\medterm{Atypical Genitalia} External genital anatomy that does not have typical male or female features, often because of differences in sex development.

\medterm{Atypical Hyperplasia of the Breast} An overgrowth of abnormal cells in breast tissue that is not cancer but may increase future breast-cancer risk.

\medterm{Atypical Teratoid Rhabdoid Tumor} A rare, aggressive cancer of the central nervous system that occurs mainly in infants and young children.

\medterm{Autoimmune Encephalitis} Brain inflammation caused by antibodies or other immune responses directed against neuronal proteins.

\medterm{Autoimmune Epilepsy} Recurrent seizures caused by an immune attack on the brain or by inflammation associated with an autoimmune disorder.

\medterm{Abdominal Regions} The nine named areas used to describe locations on the abdominal wall: the right and left hypochondriac, epigastric, right and left lumbar, umbilical, right and left iliac, and hypogastric regions.

\medterm{Abdominopelvic Cavity} The continuous body cavity below the diaphragm that contains the abdominal and pelvic organs.

\medterm{Abortifacient} A drug or substance that induces abortion by ending a pregnancy.

\medterm{Acute Rhinitis} Sudden inflammation of the nasal lining, commonly caused by a viral infection or an irritant, producing congestion, nasal discharge, and sneezing.

\medterm{Adnexa} Structures adjacent to an organ; in gynecology, the uterine adnexa include the ovaries, fallopian tubes, and supporting tissues.

\medterm{Adrenogenital Syndrome} A group of disorders, usually caused by congenital adrenal hyperplasia, in which excess adrenal androgens cause atypical genital development or early puberty.

\medterm{Afferent Neuron} A sensory nerve cell that carries impulses from receptors toward the central nervous system.

\medterm{Agranulocytes} White blood cells without prominent cytoplasmic granules; the main types are lymphocytes and monocytes.

\medterm{Airway} A passage through which air moves between the outside environment and the lungs, including the nose, mouth, pharynx, larynx, trachea, and bronchi.

\medterm{Anastomosis} A natural or surgically created connection between two tubular structures, such as blood vessels or segments of the intestine.

\medterm{Allis Clamp} A toothed surgical clamp used to grasp and hold firm tissue during an operation.

\medterm{Aphagia} Complete inability to swallow, which may result from neurologic disease, obstruction, severe pain, or loss of swallowing-muscle function.

\medterm{Apical Pulse} The heart rate measured by listening over the apex of the heart, usually at the left fifth intercostal space near the midclavicular line.

\medterm{Areola} The pigmented circular area surrounding the nipple. It contains glands that help lubricate and protect the skin during breastfeeding.

\medterm{Asthenia} Abnormal lack of physical strength or energy. It is a symptom that may occur with illness, malnutrition, or medication effects.

\medterm{Azotemia} Accumulation of nitrogen-containing waste products in the blood, usually because the kidneys are not filtering adequately.

\medterm{Atrioventricular Valves} The heart valves between the atria and ventricles: the tricuspid valve on the right and the mitral valve on the left.

\medterm{Antimicrobial Sensitivity Test} A laboratory test that determines which antimicrobial medicines inhibit or kill a microorganism isolated from a patient.

\medterm{Antideformity Position} A therapeutic positioning method that places a limb or joint in an alignment that helps prevent or limit a contracture or deformity.

\medterm{Autosomal Dominant Inheritance} Inheritance in which one pathogenic variant on a non-sex chromosome can cause a trait or disorder. An affected heterozygous parent may transmit it to about half of the children.

\medterm{Autosomal Recessive Inheritance} Inheritance in which a disorder generally requires pathogenic variants in both copies of a gene. Carrier parents typically have a one-in-four chance of an affected child in each pregnancy.

\medterm{Abdominal Angina} Postprandial abdominal pain caused by inadequate blood flow through the arteries supplying the intestines, usually because of atherosclerotic disease.

\medterm{Abdominal Binder} A wide supportive garment placed around the abdomen to provide gentle compression after surgery, injury, or selected abdominal-wall conditions.

\medterm{Abdominal Migraine} A migraine-related disorder characterized mainly by recurrent episodes of moderate to severe midline abdominal pain, often with nausea or vomiting and little or no headache.

\medterm{Abdominocentesis} Surgical or needle puncture of the abdominal wall to enter the peritoneal cavity, commonly for removal or analysis of ascitic fluid.

\medterm{Abdominal Hernia} Protrusion of an organ or tissue through a weakness or opening in the abdominal wall. Common types include incisional, umbilical, epigastric, and ventral hernias.

\medterm{Ablative Surgery} Surgery that destroys or removes abnormal tissue, often using excision, heat, cold, electrical energy, or other tissue-destructive methods.

\medterm{Afferent Loop Syndrome} Obstruction or impaired emptying of the afferent intestinal limb created by some gastric operations, causing bile and pancreatic-secretions to accumulate with pain, nausea, vomiting, or malabsorption.

\medterm{Acute Chest Syndrome} A potentially life-threatening pulmonary complication of sickle cell disease characterized by a new lung infiltrate plus fever or respiratory symptoms such as chest pain, cough, hypoxemia, or breathing difficulty.

\medterm{Acute Inflammatory Demyelinating Polyradiculoneuropathy} The most common form of Guillain--Barré syndrome, in which immune-mediated demyelination of peripheral nerves causes progressive, usually symmetric weakness and reduced reflexes.

\medterm{Acute Nephritic Syndrome} A clinical syndrome of glomerular inflammation marked by hematuria, variable proteinuria, edema, hypertension, and reduced kidney function.

\medterm{Acute Retroviral Syndrome} The acute illness that may occur shortly after HIV infection, commonly involving fever, rash, sore throat, lymph-node enlargement, muscle aches, or headache.

\medterm{Acute Transverse Myelitis} Inflammation across a segment of the spinal cord that can cause rapidly developing weakness, sensory changes, and bladder or bowel dysfunction.

\medterm{Adult Polycystic Kidney Disease} An inherited disorder, usually autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease, in which multiple kidney cysts enlarge over time and may cause hypertension, pain, hematuria, and chronic kidney failure.

\medterm{Alcohol Withdrawal Syndrome} A set of symptoms occurring after abrupt reduction or cessation of prolonged heavy alcohol use. Symptoms range from tremor, sweating, anxiety, and nausea to seizures and delirium tremens.

\medterm{Anemia of Inflammation} An anemia caused by chronic infection, inflammatory disease, or cancer, involving impaired iron availability, reduced erythropoietin response, and shortened red-cell survival.

\medterm{Anterior Cord Syndrome} A spinal-cord injury syndrome causing impaired motor function and loss of pain and temperature sensation below the lesion, with relative preservation of vibration and position sense.

\medterm{Adhesiolysis} Surgical division or removal of abnormal fibrous adhesions that bind tissues or organs together, often after inflammation, infection, surgery, or trauma.

\medterm{Abulia} Marked reduction in motivation, initiative, and spontaneous activity, usually caused by dysfunction of frontal-subcortical brain circuits or related neurologic disease.

\medterm{Acrocyanosis} Persistent, usually painless bluish discoloration and coolness of the hands or feet caused by reduced peripheral blood flow, often worsened by cold exposure.

\medterm{Adipsia} Absent or markedly reduced thirst, which can impair normal fluid intake and lead to dehydration or hypernatremia.

\medterm{Aerophagia} Excessive swallowing of air, which may cause belching, abdominal bloating, distention, or increased intestinal gas.

\medterm{Alpha Thalassemia} An inherited blood disorder caused by reduced production of alpha-globin chains of hemoglobin. Severity ranges from mild microcytosis or anemia to severe fetal or newborn disease.

\medterm{Anasarca} Severe, generalized edema involving large areas of the body, often associated with heart failure, kidney disease, liver disease, or profound hypoalbuminemia.

\medterm{Abdominal Muscles} The muscles forming the front and sides of the abdominal wall. They support the trunk, help with posture and breathing, and increase intra-abdominal pressure during coughing, lifting, and defecation.

\synonyms
Abdominal wall muscles.

\medterm{Acceptance-Based Therapies} Psychotherapeutic approaches that help a person notice and accept thoughts, feelings, or bodily sensations while reducing unhelpful attempts to control or avoid them.

\synonyms
Acceptance-based psychotherapy; acceptance-focused therapy.

\medterm{Acquisition} The process by which new information or a skill is learned and initially encoded, especially in discussions of memory and learning.

\synonyms
Learning acquisition; memory acquisition.

\medterm{Actin} A structural and contractile protein found in many cells. In muscle, actin interacts with myosin to produce contraction; in other cells, it supports shape, movement, and intracellular transport.

\synonyms
Actin filament; microfilament protein.

\medterm{Adequate Intake} A recommended average daily nutrient intake based on observed or experimentally determined levels in healthy people when evidence is insufficient to establish a recommended dietary allowance.

\synonyms
AI; adequate nutrient intake.

\medterm{Adult Day Services} Community-based daytime programs providing supervision, social activities, health-related support, or assistance with daily activities for adults who cannot safely remain alone throughout the day.

\synonyms
Adult day care; adult day program.

\medterm{Aesthetician} A trained skin-care professional who provides nonmedical cosmetic skin treatments, such as cleansing, superficial exfoliation, and selected skin-care procedures within the scope of local regulation.

\synonyms
Esthetician; skin-care specialist.

\medterm{After-Cataract} Clouding of the posterior lens capsule that develops after cataract surgery and may cause blurred or hazy vision. It is also called posterior capsule opacification.

\synonyms
Posterior capsule opacification; secondary cataract.

\medterm{Age-Related Cognitive Decline} A modest, age-associated change in memory, processing speed, or other cognitive abilities that does not necessarily interfere substantially with independent daily functioning.

\synonyms
Age-associated cognitive decline; normal cognitive aging.

\medterm{Alcohol Abuse} A nonpreferred older term for a pattern of alcohol use that causes recurrent problems or harm. Current clinical classification generally uses alcohol use disorder and specifies its severity.

\synonyms
Problem drinking; harmful alcohol use.

\medterm{Alcohol Dehydrogenase} An enzyme, found mainly in the liver, that catalyzes the first major step in ethanol metabolism by converting alcohol to acetaldehyde.

\synonyms
ADH; ethanol dehydrogenase.

\medterm{Alcohol Dependence} A pattern of alcohol use characterized by impaired control, compulsive use, tolerance, withdrawal, and continued drinking despite harm. In current terminology, it is generally classified under alcohol use disorder.

\synonyms
Alcohol use disorder; alcohol addiction; alcoholism.

\medterm{Alendronate} A bisphosphonate that reduces bone resorption and is used to treat or prevent osteoporosis and selected other disorders of excessive bone turnover.

\synonyms
Alendronic acid; oral bisphosphonate therapy.

\medterm{Alpha Cells} Endocrine cells in the pancreatic islets that produce and release glucagon, a hormone that raises blood glucose during fasting or low-glucose states.

\synonyms
Pancreatic alpha cells; glucagon-secreting cells.

\medterm{Alpha Hydroxy Acids} A group of organic acids used in dermatology and cosmetics to exfoliate the outer skin layer and improve texture or pigmentation. Examples include glycolic and lactic acid.

\synonyms
AHAs; fruit acids.

\medterm{Alpha Waves} Rhythmic electroencephalographic waves, typically around 8--13 Hz, most prominent when a relaxed, awake person has the eyes closed.

\synonyms
Alpha rhythm; posterior dominant rhythm.

\medterm{Alpha-Delta Sleep} A sleep pattern in which alpha-frequency activity intrudes into delta-wave deep sleep and may be associated with nonrestorative sleep or chronic pain.

\synonyms
Alpha-delta intrusion; alpha-delta rhythm.

\medterm{Amyloid Precursor Protein} A normal transmembrane protein that can be processed into amyloid-beta peptides, which may accumulate in the brain in Alzheimer disease.

\synonyms
APP; amyloid-beta precursor protein.

\medterm{Anterior Myocardial Infarction} A myocardial infarction involving the anterior wall of the left ventricle, usually because of obstruction in the left anterior descending coronary artery.

\synonyms
Anterior heart attack; anterior MI.

\medterm{Anti-Cyclic Citrullinated Peptide} An autoantibody directed against citrullinated proteins and used, with clinical findings and other tests, to support the diagnosis of rheumatoid arthritis.

\synonyms
Anti-CCP antibody; cyclic citrullinated peptide antibody.

\medterm{Antileukotriene} A drug class that blocks leukotriene-mediated airway inflammation or bronchoconstriction and is used in selected allergic or respiratory conditions.

\synonyms
Leukotriene modifier; leukotriene pathway inhibitor.

\medterm{Antispasmodic Drugs} Medicines that reduce smooth-muscle spasm or cramping in selected gastrointestinal, urinary, biliary, or other conditions. Choice and use depend on the cause and clinical context.

\synonyms
Antispasmodics; spasmolytics.

\medterm{Arterial Resistance} The opposition to blood flow produced mainly by the diameter and tone of small arteries and arterioles. It contributes to systemic vascular resistance and blood pressure.

\synonyms
Vascular resistance; systemic vascular resistance.

\medterm{Arterioles} Small branches of arteries that regulate blood flow into capillary beds through contraction and relaxation of their smooth muscle.

\synonyms
Resistance vessels; small arteries.

\medterm{Atheroma} A localized accumulation of lipid, inflammatory cells, and fibrous tissue within an arterial wall, forming part of an atherosclerotic plaque.

\synonyms
Atheromatous plaque; arterial plaque.

\medterm{Atherothrombotic Stroke} An ischemic stroke caused by thrombosis or artery-to-artery embolism arising from atherosclerotic disease in a large artery supplying the brain.

\synonyms
Large-artery atherosclerotic stroke; atherothrombotic ischemic stroke.

\medterm{Autologous Fat Transplant} A procedure that removes a person's own fat from one body area and transfers it to another area for soft-tissue volume restoration or contouring.

\synonyms
Autologous fat grafting; fat transfer; lipofilling.

\medterm{Axillary} Relating to the armpit or to structures located in the axilla, including axillary lymph nodes and vessels.

\synonyms
Armpit-related; pertaining to the axilla.

\medterm{Arm Pain} Pain in the shoulder, upper arm, elbow, forearm, or related tissues. Causes include muscle or tendon injury, nerve compression, arthritis, vascular disease, and referred pain; sudden severe pain with chest discomfort, weakness, or shortness of breath requires urgent assessment.

\synonyms
Pain in the arm; upper-limb pain.

\medterm{Acute flaccid myelitis (AFM)} A rare neurologic condition causing sudden weakness and loss of muscle tone, usually affecting the arms or legs and sometimes the face or breathing muscles.

\medterm{Attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) in children} A childhood neurodevelopmental disorder involving persistent inattention, hyperactivity, impulsivity, or combinations of these symptoms that impair functioning.

\medterm{Adult attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder (ADHD)} ADHD that persists into adulthood, often affecting attention, organization, impulse control, activity level, and daily functioning.

\medterm{Adnexal tumors and masses} Abnormal growths or masses involving the ovary, fallopian tube, or nearby supporting tissues; they may be benign or malignant.

\medterm{Adult Still disease} A systemic inflammatory disorder characterized by fever, joint pain or arthritis, and a transient salmon-colored rash.

\medterm{Age spots (liver spots)} Flat brown, gray, or black skin spots caused by long-term ultraviolet exposure, usually appearing on sun-exposed areas.

\medterm{Anaplastic large cell lymphoma} A rare T-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by large abnormal lymphocytes and expression of the CD30 marker; it may be systemic or skin-limited.

\medterm{Alcoholic hepatitis (Alcohol-associated hepatitis)} Inflammation and injury of the liver related to alcohol use, potentially causing jaundice, abdominal pain, fever, and liver failure.

\medterm{Amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS)} A progressive neurodegenerative disease that damages motor neurons, causing muscle weakness, loss of movement, speech or swallowing difficulty, and eventually respiratory failure.

\medterm{Aneurysms} Abnormal bulges or enlargements in weakened areas of blood-vessel walls that may enlarge, clot, dissect, or rupture.

\medterm{Angina} Chest discomfort caused by inadequate oxygen-rich blood flow to the heart muscle, usually from coronary artery disease.

\medterm{Anorgasmia in women} Persistent or recurrent difficulty achieving orgasm despite adequate sexual stimulation, causing personal distress.

\medterm{Anterior vaginal prolapse (cystocele)} Bulging of the front wall of the vagina caused by weakening of the tissue supporting the bladder, sometimes producing urinary symptoms.

\medterm{Anxiety disorders} Mental health disorders involving excessive fear, worry, or anxiety that is persistent, difficult to control, and disruptive to daily life.

\medterm{Aortic valve regurgitation} Backward leakage of blood through an incompletely closing aortic valve, which can make the heart work harder and eventually cause heart failure.

\medterm{Auditory processing disorder} Difficulty recognizing or interpreting sounds despite normal or near-normal hearing sensitivity.

\medterm{Arteriosclerosis / atherosclerosis} Arteriosclerosis is hardening and thickening of arteries; atherosclerosis is plaque buildup within artery walls that can restrict blood flow.

\medterm{Atrial septal defect (ASD)} A congenital opening between the heart's upper chambers that permits abnormal blood flow between the atria.

\medterm{Atrioventricular nodal reentry tachycardia (AVNRT)} A rapid regular heart rhythm caused by a reentrant electrical circuit involving or near the atrioventricular node.

\medterm{Atypical teratoid rhabdoid tumor (ATRT)} A rare aggressive cancer of the central nervous system, most often occurring in infants and young children.

\medterm{A guide to clinical trials for cancer} A guide to clinical trials for cancer addresses care for a guide to clinical trials for cancer, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{A guide to help children understand cancer } A guide to help children understand cancer  addresses care for a guide to help children understand cancer , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{A guide to herbal remedies} A guide to herbal remedies addresses care for a guide to herbal remedies, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{A1C test} A1C test measures glycated hemoglobin, the fraction of hemoglobin with glucose attached, to estimate average blood glucose over roughly the preceding 2 to 3 months.

\medterm{Aase syndrome} Aase syndrome is a rare inherited disorder characterized by congenital hypoplastic anemia and distinctive skeletal abnormalities.

\medterm{Abdomen - swollen} Abdomen - swollen is the presentation described by abdomen - swollen; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open} Abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open treats or supports abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open - discharge } Abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open - discharge  treats or supports abdominal aortic aneurysm repair - open - discharge ; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Abdominal CT scan} Abdominal CT scan evaluates abdominal ct scan; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abdominal exploration} Surgical inspection of the abdominal cavity to identify or treat a suspected source of pain, bleeding, infection, obstruction, or other disease.

\medterm{Abdominal MRI scan} Abdominal MRI scan evaluates abdominal mri scan; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abdominal pain - children under age 12} Abdominal pain - children under age 12 is the presentation described by abdominal pain - children under age 12; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Abdominal radiation - discharge} Abdominal radiation - discharge evaluates abdominal radiation - discharge; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abdominal sounds} Sounds produced by movement of gas and fluid through the intestines; changes in frequency or character may support, but do not establish, a diagnosis.

\medterm{Abdominal tap } Abdominal tap  is a treatment, procedure, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Abdominal thrusts} First-aid maneuver used for severe choking in a conscious person, intended to expel an obstructing object from the airway.

\medterm{Abdominal ultrasound} Abdominal ultrasound evaluates abdominal ultrasound; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abdominal wall fat pad biopsy} Abdominal wall fat pad biopsy evaluates abdominal wall fat pad biopsy; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abdominal wall surgery} Abdominal wall surgery treats or supports abdominal wall surgery; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Abnormal hemoglobins testing} Abnormal hemoglobins testing evaluates abnormal hemoglobins testing; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Abnormally dark or light skin} Abnormally dark or light skin is the presentation described by abnormally dark or light skin; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Abortion - medication} Medication abortion uses prescribed medicines, commonly mifepristone followed by misoprostol or misoprostol alone, to end an early intrauterine pregnancy; confirmation of completion and assessment for ectopic pregnancy are important.

\medterm{Abortion - procedure} Abortion - procedure treats or supports abortion - procedure; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Abortion - procedure - aftercare} Abortion - procedure - aftercare treats or supports abortion - procedure - aftercare; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Abscess - abdomen or pelvis} A localized collection of pus within the abdominal or pelvic cavity, usually caused by infection or leakage from an injured or inflamed organ. It may cause pain, fever, and sepsis and often requires antimicrobial treatment and drainage.

\medterm{Absence of sweating} Absence of sweating is the presentation described by absence of sweating; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Absent menstrual periods - primary} Primary amenorrhea, meaning failure to have the first menstrual period by the expected age. Causes include chromosomal or reproductive-tract abnormalities, endocrine disorders, chronic disease, and inadequate energy availability.

\medterm{Absent menstrual periods - secondary} Secondary amenorrhea, meaning absence of menstrual periods for an extended interval in a person who previously menstruated. Pregnancy is considered first; other causes include endocrine disease, medications, stress, weight change, and ovarian dysfunction.

\medterm{Absent pulmonary valve} Absent pulmonary valve is a congenital heart defect in which the pulmonary valve is missing or severely underdeveloped, often causing pulmonary-artery enlargement and respiratory symptoms.

\medterm{ACE blood test} ACE blood test measures angiotensin-converting enzyme in serum; it may support evaluation of sarcoidosis and selected granulomatous or liver disorders but is not diagnostic alone.

\medterm{ACE inhibitors} ACE inhibitors block formation of angiotensin II and are used for hypertension, heart failure, diabetic kidney protection, and selected cardiovascular conditions.

\medterm{Acetaminophen and codeine overdose} Acetaminophen and codeine overdose involves toxic exposure to acetaminophen and codeine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Acetaminophen dosing for children} Weight-based dosing guidance for acetaminophen in children; the total daily dose must account for all combination products to avoid liver toxicity.

\medterm{Acetaminophen overdose} Acetaminophen overdose involves toxic exposure to acetaminophen overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Acetone poisoning} Acetone poisoning involves toxic exposure to acetone poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Acetylcholine receptor antibody} Acetylcholine receptor antibody testing detects antibodies associated with autoimmune myasthenia gravis and is interpreted with the neurologic examination and other studies.

\medterm{Aches and pains during pregnancy} Aches and pains during pregnancy is the presentation described by aches and pains during pregnancy; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Achilles tendon repair} Achilles tendon repair reconnects a ruptured Achilles tendon, followed by immobilization and graduated rehabilitation to restore plantar-flexion strength.

\medterm{Achilles tendon rupture - aftercare} Achilles tendon rupture - aftercare addresses care for achilles tendon rupture - aftercare, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Acid loading test (pH)} A test of the kidney’s ability to excrete acid after an acid load, used in evaluating renal tubular acidification disorders.

\medterm{Acid mucopolysaccharides} An older term for glycosaminoglycans, long carbohydrate chains in connective-tissue substances such as cartilage, skin, and synovial fluid. Abnormal degradation or storage can cause mucopolysaccharidoses.

\medterm{Acid soldering flux poisoning} Acid soldering flux poisoning involves toxic exposure to acid soldering flux poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Acid-fast stain} Acid-fast stain uses dyes that remain in the cell wall of mycobacteria and some related organisms, helping identify them in clinical specimens.

\medterm{ACL reconstruction - discharge } ACL reconstruction - discharge  treats or supports acl reconstruction - discharge ; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Acne - self-care} Acne - self-care addresses care for acne - self-care, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Acquired platelet function defect} Acquired platelet function defect is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Acrodysostosis} Acrodysostosis is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{ACTH blood test} ACTH blood test evaluates acth blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Acute adrenal crisis} Acute adrenal crisis is life-threatening cortisol deficiency causing hypotension, vomiting, abdominal pain, electrolyte disturbance, hypoglycemia, or shock; urgent glucocorticoid and fluid treatment is required.

\medterm{Acute arterial occlusion - kidney} Acute arterial occlusion of the kidney abruptly reduces renal blood flow and may cause flank pain, hematuria, hypertension, or acute kidney injury.

\medterm{Acute cerebellar ataxia} Acute cerebellar ataxia is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL)} Acute lymphoblastic leukemia is a rapidly progressive cancer of immature lymphoid precursor cells that crowds the bone marrow and can involve blood, lymph nodes, or the central nervous system.

\medterm{Acute myeloid leukemia (AML) - children} Childhood acute myeloid leukemia is a marrow and blood cancer caused by uncontrolled proliferation of immature myeloid cells, producing anemia, infection, and bleeding risk.

\medterm{Acute myeloid leukemia - adult} Adult acute myeloid leukemia is an aggressive malignancy of myeloid blood-cell precursors that impairs normal blood-cell production.

\medterm{Adenoid removal} Adenoid removal treats or supports adenoid removal; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Adolescent development} Adolescent development is the presentation described by adolescent development; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Adolescent test or procedure preparation} Adolescent test or procedure preparation treats or supports adolescent test or procedure preparation; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Adrenal gland removal} Adrenal gland removal treats or supports adrenal gland removal; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Adrenal glands} Adrenal glands sit above the kidneys and produce cortisol, aldosterone, adrenal androgens, and catecholamines that regulate stress, blood pressure, salt balance, and metabolism.

\medterm{Adrenergic bronchodilator overdose} Adrenergic bronchodilator overdose involves toxic exposure to adrenergic bronchodilator overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Adult soft tissue sarcoma} Adult soft tissue sarcoma is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Advance care directives} Documents and instructions that state a person’s healthcare preferences and designate a surrogate decision-maker if the person later lacks decision-making capacity.

\medterm{Aerobic bacteria} Bacteria that grow using oxygen or tolerate oxygen, although individual species differ in their exact oxygen requirements.

\medterm{Aflatoxin} Aflatoxin is a toxin produced by certain Aspergillus molds in contaminated foods; chronic exposure can injure the liver and increase hepatocellular-carcinoma risk.

\medterm{After an exposure to sharps or body fluids} Post-exposure evaluation after possible contact with blood or body fluids, including wound care, source assessment, and timely consideration of HIV, hepatitis B, or hepatitis C prevention and testing.

\medterm{After chemotherapy - discharge} After chemotherapy - discharge addresses care for after chemotherapy - discharge, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Aicardi syndrome} Aicardi syndrome is a rare developmental brain disorder classically involving agenesis of the corpus callosum, chorioretinal lacunae, and infantile seizures.

\medterm{Albumin blood (serum) test} Albumin blood (serum) test evaluates albumin blood (serum) test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Alcohol and pregnancy} Alcohol crosses the placenta and can cause fetal alcohol spectrum disorders; no known amount or timing during pregnancy is established as safe.

\medterm{Aldosterone blood test} Aldosterone blood test evaluates aldosterone blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - adult} Allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - adult addresses care for allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - adult, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - child} Allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - child addresses care for allergic rhinitis - what to ask your doctor - child, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Allergies, asthma, and dust} Dust can carry allergens such as dust-mite particles that trigger allergic rhinitis or asthma in susceptible people. Symptoms may include sneezing, congestion, wheezing, cough, and shortness of breath.

\medterm{Allergies, asthma, and molds} Mold spores and fragments can trigger allergic rhinitis, conjunctivitis, or asthma in sensitized people. Reducing exposure and treating the specific allergic or airway condition are guided by clinical assessment.

\medterm{Allergies, asthma, and pollen} Pollen is a seasonal airborne allergen that can trigger allergic rhinitis, conjunctivitis, or asthma. Typical symptoms include sneezing, itchy eyes, nasal congestion, cough, and wheezing.

\medterm{Allergy shots} Subcutaneous allergen immunotherapy involving gradually increasing doses of selected allergens to reduce allergic sensitivity; administration requires monitoring for systemic reactions.

\medterm{Allergy testing - skin} Allergy testing - skin evaluates allergy testing - skin; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Aloe} A plant-derived substance used in some topical products and supplements; topical preparations may soothe minor irritation, while oral latex-containing products can cause cramps, diarrhea, and electrolyte problems.

\medterm{ALP - blood test} ALP - blood test evaluates alp - blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{ALP isoenzyme test} ALP isoenzyme test evaluates alp isoenzyme test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Alpha-1 antitrypsin blood test} Alpha-1 antitrypsin blood test evaluates alpha-1 antitrypsin blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{ALS - resources} ALS - resources addresses care for als - resources, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Alstrom syndrome} A rare autosomal-recessive disorder caused by ALMS1 variants, associated with cone-rod dystrophy, hearing loss, obesity, insulin resistance, cardiomyopathy, and progressive kidney or liver disease.

\medterm{Alternative medicine - pain relief} Alternative medicine - pain relief addresses care for alternative medicine - pain relief, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Alveolar abnormalities} Structural or functional changes in the lung alveoli that can impair gas exchange and occur with infection, edema, emphysema, interstitial disease, or developmental disorders.

\medterm{Alzheimer disease - resources} Alzheimer disease resources provide education about progressive cognitive decline, caregiver support, safety, advance planning, and available treatment and services.

\medterm{Am I in labor?} Am I in labor? is the presentation described by am i in labor?; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Amino acids} Amino acids are organic molecules that form proteins and also serve as precursors or fuels in human metabolism.

\medterm{Aminoaciduria} Aminoaciduria is abnormal urinary loss of amino acids caused by renal tubular transport defects, inherited metabolic disease, or generalized tubular injury.

\medterm{Aminophylline overdose} Aminophylline overdose involves toxic exposure to aminophylline overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Amitriptyline and perphenazine overdose} Amitriptyline and perphenazine overdose involves toxic exposure to amitriptyline and perphenazine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Amitriptyline hydrochloride overdose} Amitriptyline hydrochloride overdose involves toxic exposure to amitriptyline hydrochloride overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Ammonia blood test} Ammonia blood test evaluates ammonia blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Ammonia poisoning} Ammonia poisoning involves toxic exposure to ammonia poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Ammonium hydroxide poisoning} Ammonium hydroxide poisoning involves toxic exposure to ammonium hydroxide poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Amniotic band sequence} Amniotic band sequence results when fibrous amniotic strands entangle developing fetal parts, producing constriction rings, limb defects, or craniofacial and body-wall abnormalities.

\medterm{Amylase - blood} A blood test measuring amylase, an enzyme produced mainly by the pancreas and salivary glands; elevated values may occur in pancreatitis and other conditions.

\medterm{Amylase - urine} A urine test measuring amylase excretion, sometimes used with blood amylase when evaluating pancreatic or salivary-gland disease.

\medterm{Anal itching - self-care} Anal itching - self-care addresses care for anal itching - self-care, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Anemia caused by low iron - children} Anemia caused by low iron - children is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Anemia caused by low iron - infants and toddlers} Anemia caused by low iron - infants and toddlers is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - adult} Anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - adult addresses care for anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - adult, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - child} Anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - child addresses care for anesthesia - what to ask your doctor - child, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Aneurysm in the brain} A brain aneurysm is a focal weakness and ballooning of a cerebral-artery wall; rupture causes subarachnoid hemorrhage and sudden severe headache.

\medterm{Angina - discharge} Angina - discharge addresses care for angina - discharge, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Angina - what to ask your doctor } Angina - what to ask your doctor  addresses care for angina - what to ask your doctor , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Angina - when you have chest pain} Angina with chest pain reflects transient myocardial ischemia; new, severe, prolonged, or rest pain may signal acute coronary syndrome and requires emergency assessment.

\medterm{Angiodysplasia of the colon} A degenerative abnormality of small blood vessels in the colon that can cause intermittent or recurrent lower gastrointestinal bleeding, often without pain. Diagnosis and treatment depend on the bleeding pattern and endoscopic findings.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent - heart - discharge } Angioplasty and stent - heart - discharge  addresses care for angioplasty and stent - heart - discharge , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent placement - carotid artery} Angioplasty and stent placement - carotid artery is a treatment, procedure, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent placement - carotid artery - discharge } Angioplasty and stent placement - carotid artery - discharge  addresses care for angioplasty and stent placement - carotid artery - discharge , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent placement - heart} Angioplasty and stent placement - heart is a treatment, procedure, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent placement - peripheral arteries} Angioplasty and stent placement - peripheral arteries is a treatment, procedure, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Angioplasty and stent placement - peripheral arteries - discharge } Angioplasty and stent placement - peripheral arteries - discharge  addresses care for angioplasty and stent placement - peripheral arteries - discharge , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Ankle arthroscopy} Ankle arthroscopy treats or supports ankle arthroscopy; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Ankle fracture - aftercare} Ankle fracture - aftercare addresses care for ankle fracture - aftercare, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Ankle replacement} Ankle replacement treats or supports ankle replacement; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Ankle replacement - discharge } Ankle replacement - discharge  treats or supports ankle replacement - discharge ; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Ankle sprain - aftercare} Ankle sprain - aftercare addresses care for ankle sprain - aftercare, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Anomalous left coronary artery from the pulmonary artery} A rare congenital anomaly in which the left coronary artery arises from the pulmonary artery instead of the aorta. It can cause myocardial ischemia, heart failure, arrhythmia, or sudden death, particularly in infancy or exertion.

\medterm{Anorchia} Anorchia is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Anoscopy and high-resolution anoscopy} Anoscopy and high-resolution anoscopy evaluates anoscopy and high-resolution anoscopy; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) injury} Anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) injury is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) injury - aftercare} Anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) injury - aftercare addresses care for anterior cruciate ligament (acl) injury - aftercare, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Anterior knee pain} Anterior knee pain is the presentation described by anterior knee pain; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Anterior vaginal wall repair} Anterior vaginal wall repair treats or supports anterior vaginal wall repair; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Anthrax blood test} Anthrax blood test evaluates anthrax blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anti-DNase B blood test} Anti-DNase B blood test evaluates anti-dnase b blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anti-glomerular basement membrane blood test} Anti-glomerular basement membrane blood test evaluates anti-glomerular basement membrane blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anti-glomerular basement membrane disease} Anti-glomerular basement membrane disease is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Anti-insulin antibody test} Anti-insulin antibody test evaluates anti-insulin antibody test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anti-reflux surgery - children} Anti-reflux surgery - children treats or supports anti-reflux surgery - children; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Anti-reflux surgery - children - discharge } Anti-reflux surgery - children - discharge  treats or supports anti-reflux surgery - children - discharge ; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Anti-reflux surgery - discharge } Anti-reflux surgery - discharge  treats or supports anti-reflux surgery - discharge ; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Anti-rust product poisoning} Anti-rust product poisoning involves toxic exposure to anti-rust product poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Anti-smooth muscle antibody} An autoantibody associated especially with autoimmune hepatitis, although low levels can occur in other autoimmune or infectious conditions.

\medterm{Antibody titer blood test} Antibody titer blood test evaluates antibody titer blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Anticoagulant rodenticides poisoning} Anticoagulant rodenticides poisoning involves toxic exposure to anticoagulant rodenticides poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Antidiarrheal medicine overdose} Antidiarrheal medicine overdose involves toxic exposure to antidiarrheal medicine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Antidiuretic hormone blood test} Antidiuretic hormone blood test evaluates antidiuretic hormone blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Antifreeze poisoning} Antifreeze poisoning involves toxic exposure to antifreeze poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Antihistamines for allergies} Medicines that block histamine effects and reduce symptoms such as itching, sneezing, and runny nose; sedation and interactions vary by agent.

\medterm{Antimitochondrial antibody} An autoantibody strongly associated with primary biliary cholangitis, interpreted with cholestatic liver tests and clinical findings.

\medterm{Antinuclear antibody panel} Antinuclear antibody panel evaluates antinuclear antibody panel; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Antiparietal cell antibody test} Antiparietal cell antibody test evaluates antiparietal cell antibody test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Antiphospholipid syndrome - APS} Antiphospholipid syndrome is an autoimmune thrombophilia associated with venous or arterial clots and pregnancy morbidity in the presence of persistent antiphospholipid antibodies.

\medterm{Antiplatelet medicines - P2Y12 inhibitors} Antiplatelet drugs such as clopidogrel, prasugrel, and ticagrelor that reduce ADP-mediated platelet activation and thrombosis risk, with bleeding as a major adverse effect.

\medterm{Antithrombin III blood test} Antithrombin III blood test evaluates antithrombin iii blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Antithyroglobulin antibody test} Antithyroglobulin antibody test evaluates antithyroglobulin antibody test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular} Aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular treats or supports aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular - discharge} Aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular - discharge treats or supports aortic aneurysm repair - endovascular - discharge; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Aortic angiography} Aortic angiography evaluates aortic angiography; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Aortic arch syndrome} Aortic arch syndrome is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Aortic valve surgery - minimally invasive} Aortic valve surgery - minimally invasive treats or supports aortic valve surgery - minimally invasive; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Aortic valve surgery - open} Aortic valve surgery - open treats or supports aortic valve surgery - open; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Aplastic} Describing failure of a tissue, especially bone marrow, to produce adequate cells; aplastic anemia causes pancytopenia from marrow failure.

\medterm{Apolipoprotein B100} The structural apolipoprotein on atherogenic particles such as LDL and VLDL; its concentration reflects the number of potentially atherogenic lipoprotein particles.

\medterm{Apolipoprotein CII} A cofactor required to activate lipoprotein lipase, the enzyme that hydrolyzes triglycerides in circulating lipoproteins.

\medterm{Apoplexy} An older term for sudden loss of neurologic function, historically referring to stroke and sometimes used for hemorrhage or infarction in an organ.

\medterm{Appetite - decreased} Appetite - decreased is the presentation described by appetite - decreased; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Appetite - increased} Appetite - increased is the presentation described by appetite - increased; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Appropriate for gestational age (AGA)} Describing a fetus or newborn whose estimated weight or size falls within the expected reference range for the gestational age, commonly between the 10th and 90th percentiles.

\medterm{Arachnodactyly} Arachnodactyly is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Are you getting too much exercise?} Are you getting too much exercise? is the presentation described by are you getting too much exercise?; onset, severity, associated findings, causes, and need for care guide assessment.

\medterm{Arm CT scan} Arm CT scan evaluates arm ct scan; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Arm MRI scan} Arm MRI scan evaluates arm mri scan; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Arrhythmias} Abnormal heart rhythms caused by altered impulse formation or conduction; they range from harmless premature beats to rhythms that impair circulation.

\medterm{Arterial embolism} Arterial embolism is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Arterial stick} Needle puncture of an artery, usually to obtain blood for arterial blood-gas or related testing; bleeding and arterial injury are possible complications.

\medterm{Arteriogram} Arteriogram evaluates arteriogram; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Arthritis - resources} Arthritis - resources addresses care for arthritis - resources, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Artificial urinary sphincter} An implanted device that restores urinary continence by allowing controlled compression and release of the urethra. It is used mainly for selected cases of stress urinary incontinence and requires surgical placement and follow-up.

\medterm{Aspartate aminotransferase (AST) blood test} Aspartate aminotransferase (AST) blood test evaluates aspartate aminotransferase (ast) blood test; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Aspartic acid} A nonessential amino acid incorporated into proteins and involved in the urea cycle, nucleotide synthesis, and other metabolic pathways. It is not the same as aspartame, an artificial sweetener that contains aspartic acid.

\medterm{Aspergillosis precipitin} An antibody test that may support evaluation of exposure or immune response to Aspergillus; results must be interpreted with symptoms, imaging, and other tests.

\medterm{Asphalt cement poisoning} Asphalt cement poisoning involves toxic exposure to asphalt cement poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Aspirin and heart disease} Aspirin inhibits platelet aggregation and may prevent recurrent cardiovascular events in selected patients; routine primary prevention requires individualized assessment of benefit and bleeding risk.

\medterm{Aspirin overdose} Aspirin overdose involves toxic exposure to aspirin overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Assisted delivery with forceps} Assisted delivery with forceps treats or supports assisted delivery with forceps; indication, preparation, complications, and recovery depend on the procedure and patient.

\medterm{Asthma - child - discharge} Asthma - child - discharge addresses care for asthma - child - discharge, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Asthma - control drugs} Long-term asthma medicines, especially inhaled corticosteroids, reduce airway inflammation and prevent exacerbations; they do not replace a rapid-relief medicine for acute symptoms.

\medterm{Asthma - quick-relief drugs } Rapid-relief asthma medicines, commonly inhaled short-acting beta-2 agonists, relax bronchial smooth muscle for prompt symptom relief; frequent use signals inadequate control.

\medterm{Asthma and allergy resources} Asthma and allergy resources addresses care for asthma and allergy resources, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Asthma and school} School-based asthma care includes an action plan, access to prescribed rescue medicine, trigger reduction, and staff training to recognize worsening breathing.

\medterm{Asthma in adults - what to ask the doctor} Asthma in adults - what to ask the doctor addresses care for asthma in adults - what to ask the doctor, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Asthma in children} A chronic inflammatory airway disorder in children causing variable wheeze, cough, chest tightness, and shortness of breath; diagnosis and treatment are guided by age-appropriate history and testing.

\medterm{Asthma in children - what to ask your doctor} Asthma in children - what to ask your doctor addresses care for asthma in children - what to ask your doctor, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Asymptomatic bacteriuria} Bacteria detected in urine without urinary symptoms; treatment is selective, such as during pregnancy or before certain urologic procedures.

\medterm{Asymptomatic HIV infection} HIV infection without current symptoms; ongoing viral replication can damage immunity, so diagnosis, monitoring, and antiretroviral treatment remain important.

\medterm{Atopic dermatitis - children - homecare} Atopic dermatitis - children - homecare addresses care for atopic dermatitis - children - homecare, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Atopic dermatitis - self-care} Atopic dermatitis - self-care addresses care for atopic dermatitis - self-care, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Atrial fibrillation - discharge } Atrial fibrillation - discharge  addresses care for atrial fibrillation - discharge , including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Atrial fibrillation and atrial flutter} Atrial fibrillation produces chaotic atrial activity, while atrial flutter produces a rapid organized circuit; both can cause palpitations and increase stroke risk.

\medterm{Atrial myxoma} A usually benign primary heart tumor, most often in the left atrium, that may obstruct blood flow or produce emboli and constitutional symptoms.

\medterm{Aural polyps} Abnormal tissue projections in the ear canal or middle ear, often associated with chronic infection, inflammation, or cholesteatoma and requiring otologic evaluation.

\medterm{Autism - resources} Autism - resources addresses care for autism - resources, including practical management, warning signs, follow-up, and questions for a clinician.

\medterm{Autism spectrum disorder - Asperger syndrome} Autism spectrum disorder - Asperger syndrome is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Autism spectrum disorder - childhood disintegrative disorder} Childhood disintegrative disorder, also called Heller syndrome, is a rare condition involving marked loss of previously acquired language, social, and adaptive skills after apparently typical early development. Modern classifications place it within autism spectrum disorder, and evaluation should exclude other neurologic causes.

\medterm{Autoerythrocyte sensitivity} A rare syndrome of painful bruising or purpura attributed to an abnormal reaction to a person’s own red-cell components. The diagnosis is one of exclusion after bleeding disorders, medication effects, and other causes of bruising are assessed.

\medterm{Autoimmune diseases} Autoimmune diseases arise when immune responses target the body’s own tissues, producing organ-specific or systemic inflammation and damage.

\medterm{Autoimmune liver disease panel} Autoimmune liver disease panel evaluates autoimmune liver disease panel; interpretation uses the method, specimen or body region, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Autoinflammatory diseases} Autoinflammatory diseases is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Autoinoculation} Transfer of an infectious agent from one body site to another on the same person, often by touching or scratching.

\medterm{Automatic dishwasher soap poisoning} Automatic dishwasher soap poisoning involves toxic exposure to automatic dishwasher soap poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and antidote availability guide the clinical response.

\medterm{Autosomal dominant} Autosomal dominant inheritance occurs when one altered copy of a gene can produce a trait or disorder; an affected heterozygous parent may transmit it to about half of offspring.

\medterm{Autosomal dominant tubulointerstitial kidney disease} Autosomal dominant tubulointerstitial kidney disease is a condition or finding involving the named organ or process; diagnosis depends on its characteristic features, causes, severity, and appropriate examination or testing.

\medterm{Autosomal recessive} Autosomal recessive inheritance generally requires pathogenic variants in both copies of a gene; unaffected carrier parents can have an affected child.

\medterm{Axillary nerve dysfunction} Impairment of the axillary nerve, often from shoulder trauma, dislocation, compression, or surgery. It can cause weakness of shoulder abduction, deltoid wasting, and numbness over the lateral shoulder.

\medterm{A Hemophilia (Hemophilia)} Alternate index label for Hemophilia.

\medterm{AA (Alopecia Areata)} Alternate index label for Alopecia Areata.

\medterm{AAA (Abdominal Aortic Aneurysm)} Alternate index label for Abdominal Aortic Aneurysm.

\medterm{Abbreviations Medical Terms (Common Medical Abbreviations and Terms)} Alternate index label for Common Medical Abbreviations and Terms.

\medterm{Abdominal Pain (Causes, Remedies, Treatment)} Alternate index label for Causes, Remedies, Treatment.

\medterm{Abnormal Heart Rhythms (Heart Rhythm Disorders)} Alternate index label for Heart Rhythm Disorders.

\medterm{Abortion Spontaneous (Miscarriage Causes and Symptoms)} Alternate index label for Miscarriage Causes and Symptoms.

\medterm{Abscessed Tooth Guide} An abscessed tooth contains a localized collection of pus caused by bacterial infection, usually from decay, a crack, or gum disease.
